Li e Sergeant-Ifajor of the 4th Battalion Prince of

Wslesfe Volunteers (South lones,hire Regiment)

There is no more popular figure in local Territorial circles than Mr. 0. H. Ingram, who has thin week retired from the rank ii sergeant major of the 4th Battalion Prince rf We're's \ clut.teers (S.ruth Lance- shite Regiment) with a view, it is tiuders.ood, to becoming quartermi,ster of the Battalion. Mr. Ingram has WO twenty-eight years military service, and doting his association with the local Territorials his

col ectenticus attentirn to duty and genial good 'humour have gained the high regard of officers end men alike. Ile joined tho Rifle Brigade in 1883 at Aldershot, transferring to the South Lanenshire Regi- meet in 18.90. Mr. Ingrain has hail coneiderable foieign service in India, Egypt and South Africa He went to the latter C deny twice—in 1885 and again in the Boer War, when he accompanied the :Ird South Lancashire Regiment. On returning he joined the permanent Mello( the local Volunteer battelion as mounted infantry instructor in 1002. Ile was eppointed eergeent•major in 1905 in euccesaion to Sergeant Major Rawlinson, who proceedel to Stock- port ; and he has devoted himself wholmbeartedly to the interests of the battalion, being prominent in evety scheme to increase We strength and efficiency. A striking tribute to his stork was paid by a special military correepondeut of "'the Timee," who visited the Volunteer camp at Llanbeiis in August, Vet:, and wrote—' The bat V B. South lAncashile Regiment is a very gred example of what a volunteer battalion can become with tapable leadership and administration It can turn out a battalion 1,010 strong at all times including 711 N.C.O.'s and RINI between the ages of Si) and 35. The materiel is very good indeed and there ale not half a does men in the corps whose absence one would art regret judging them lry the eye as they stand on parade. . . . . . Whether in the regular army or in the auxiliary forces, we hate never yet recognized the value of the position I l•e commanding m fficer nor rewarded good work done in peace in the manner it deserves. What, after all, is the canes if microns in war hot g oil work done in pence? The allotment of a small number of honhurs every year to brigadiers and commanding officer, who have worked up their commands to a high standard of efficiency would pro- duce a good effect so long ne honour, are solely granted for efficiency and not for any other cause. There are also a number of admirable N and warrant officers of the permanent staff, like Sergeant- Major Ingram. of the tat V.B. South Lancashire Regiment, of whose services to the second line it is difficult to speak too highly. We ought to look after these people since they are doing a great work and the best of the Volunteers could not lift•e reached their present standard without them." • Mr. Ingram holds the long sorrier medal and the South Aftican medal with three clasps, line of the more ro cent Territorial projects in which he took a very active and much appreciated part was the torch. light tattoo held in Victoria Park, which provided the people of Wanington with a novel and impressive spectisele.We are sure we are voicing the opinion of Warrington Territorials, as well as of a large body of the general townspeople, when we express the hope that Hr. logrant's connection with the local battalion in his new capacity will be a long and prosperous one We understand that Mr Ingram will be succeeded as sergeant-major hy Instructor Newell.

THE CORONATION. IMPORTANT CHANGE IN LOCAL PROGRAMME. No Children's Festivities on Coronation Day.

We understand that there will be an alteration in the programme of the Coronation festivities. It was originally proposed to assemble and entertain all the elementary rchool children of the Borough in Vico ris Park, and the Mayor enme time ago called a meeting of the head teachers to assist in devising a programme. Thi-t meeting appointed a tub-committee to consider and report upon the matt. r. The Sub Committee proposed that the celebration should take the form of a aeries of tableaux to be per- formed by the children and also that old Engliah games such as Monis and Maypole It:aloes should be given. A further meeting of the head teachers was hehl at the Town Hall oil the Ist. instant, and a great number of objections were taken to the proposals of the Sub- Committee, which were not adopted. A steeling of the Coronation Sub-Committee was held immediately afterwards and the following are their proceedings the Town Clerk reported that a meeting of the Heed Teachers I.ad been held at the Town Hall this day, and the Mayor reported thereon. Resolved, l hat haring rogaid to the objection. of Heed Teachers to the suggestions made by their Sub-Contreittee appoitind to consider the form of celefirati a of the Coronation by the school children of the Town and to the following resolution palmed by them That this meeting of Head Teachers respect- fully seggest to the Coronation Committee that the celebration of the Coronation as far as the children are cencerned shall be held in four or more ceetiee. Your Sub Committee are very reluctantly com- pelled to recommend that the proposed massing of the cbildien in Victoria Park be abandoned, recognising that it is almost impossible to carry out such a demonstration without the whole- hearted co-operation and assistance of the teachers of the town. Your SulcCommittee after careful comideration are not prepared to recommend the adoption of the four proposed centres. These resolutions were confirmed by the full Coronation Committee at a meeting held on Wednes- day, and we undreamed that the proposed cereimey in Victoria Park will accordingly not take place.

The souvenirs of the occaelo'n which Were to be given to the children will he distributed at the schools on the day before the Coronation, and the Mayor will personally •isit each school. The Committee have also decided that bands shall play during Thursday afternoon and evening in Vict..ria and Bank
